# What kppc compiles: the specification suite

Generated by `compiler\tests\spec.cmd` from `compiler/tests/spec/<area>/*.kpp`: one small program per
construct, compiled, run and compared with its `.expected` output. The Factorio column is the
construct's count in the game's sources (`src`, `CommonUtil`, `Agui`; 7,371 files), so the gaps are
ordered by what the conversion needs. A pending row is a known gap and shows the compiler's first
complaint; it turns into a failure of the suite the day it starts passing, so the row gets removed.

**1528 pass, 6 pending, 0 failing, 0 fixed** (1534 snippets, 499.1 s).

## Language: statements, expressions, declarations

| Snippet | Construct | Status | Factorio | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `language/adjacent_invokes_cleanup_once_release.kpp` | a call that throws runs only its own scope's cleanups, even when the next call's scope starts at its return address (release) | pass |  |  |
| `language/alias_array_function_types.kpp` | aliases of array and function types: `using Row = int[3]`, `using Grid = size_t[2][3]`, `using Unary = double(double)` | pass |  |  |
| `language/alignas.kpp` | alignas on a struct | pass | 68 alignas/alignof uses in 15 files |  |
| `language/alignas_on_variables_and_members.kpp` | alignas on a variable or a data member: a global, a static and a local start at a multiple of the alignment, and a member does within its class, whose alignment it raises; a class's raw storage `AlignedBytes<sizeof(T), alignof(T)>` for an over-aligned T has T's alignment | pass | Atlas.cpp's `alignas(16) uint32_t b[4]` (read with _mm_load_si128, which faults when misaligned), YCoCgDXTCompression's 15 `alignas(16) static dword SIMD_SSE2_...[4]` constants, the library's raw storage for Optional<T> |  |

| `language/alloca.kpp` | alloca (<malloc.h>, MSVC's _alloca): a block of a run-time size on the stack of the calling function, distinct per call and aligned to 16 bytes, which stays valid until that function returns, also when the call is in a loop or in a block that has ended; the function's return frees all of them, so calling it again and again does not grow the stack | pass | libraries\vorbis (63 uses in 13 files: block.c's `float *lpc=alloca(order*sizeof(*lpc));`), libraries\opus (USE_ALLOCA: celt and silk's ALLOC), libraries\imgui (10) |  |

Expected output from a C++ twin built with VS clang++ |  |

So `u3 - 2` is the int -1, `-u3`, `~u3` and `u3 << 31` are ints, `(w3 - 3) / 2` is 0, `no ? u3 : -1` is -1, the comparisons are of ints, sizeof(i20 + 0) is 4, a compound assignment computes in int (`u3 /= -2` from 6: -3, its low 3 bits 5), in a member function and at compile time alike; clang prints the same (g++ the same but for the conditional, 4294967295: it keeps a bit-field operand of ?: unpromoted) | pass |  |  |

| `language/cast_casts_away_no_const.kpp` | static_cast, reinterpret_cast and dynamic_cast cast away no const ([expr.static.cast] 1, [expr.reinterpret.cast] 2, [expr.dynamic.cast] 2): `static_cast<S&&>(s)` and `static_cast<int&>(c)` of a const object, `static_cast<S*>(p)` and `reinterpret_cast<int*>(p)` of a `const S*`, `static_cast<D&>(b)` of a `const S&` and `static_cast<int*>(v)` of a `const void*` are compile errors, where const_cast and a C-style cast may, and a cast that keeps the const (`static_cast<const D&>(b)`) or makes a copy (`static_cast<S>(s)`) casts none away; an rvalue reference binds no const object either, `S&& r = move(s);` and `take(move(s))` with `take(S&&)` ([dcl.init.ref] 5) | pass |  |  |
| `language/cast_of_parenthesised_cast.kpp` | a C-style cast to a type a typedef names, of a C-style cast to a builtin type: `(HANDLE)(long long)-1` is `(HANDLE)((long long)-1)`, since a parenthesised builtin type is never a call's argument list | pass | the converter's Windows code (`(HANDLE)(long long)-1` for INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE); windows.h's own `((HANDLE)(LONG_PTR)-1)` comes through the C import |  |
| `language/cast_selects_overload_by_its_type.kpp` | a cast of an overloaded function's name to a pointer to function picks the overload of that type, as C++'s [over.over] has it for an explicit conversion: `static_cast<long long (*)(long long)>(twice)` is the long long one, a C-style cast `(double (*)(double))twice` and `static_cast<double (*)(double)>(&twice)` the double one, also as a function template's argument; clang prints the same | pass |  |  |
| `language/cast_to_alias_of_parenthesised.kpp` | a C-style cast to a typedef or alias name of a parenthesised operand: `(Packed)(255) << 24` casts 255, it does not call `(Packed)` | pass | imgui's IM_COL32 at its four uses in DebugTools, as the converter expands it: `(((ImU32)(255)<<IM_COL32_A_SHIFT) | ((ImU32)(180)<<IM_COL32_B_SHIFT) | ...)` |  |
| `language/chained_comparison_is_error.kpp` | a comparison of a comparison without parentheses is an error (kovarex, 2026-11-04; design.md 22): `a < b > c`, which C++ reads as `(a < b) > c` and a reader as a template-id, `a < b < c`, `a == b != c`; parenthesized, or a relational inside an equality (`a < b == c < d`), it is as C++ | pass |  |  |
| `language/char32_t_is_unsigned_on_every_target.kpp` | char32_t is an unsigned 32-bit type of its own on every target, as C++ has it: its greatest value is 2^32 - 1, it is not signed, it is not wchar_t, and in a comparison with an int it promotes to unsigned int (C++ [conv.prom] 2: int cannot hold every value), so its greatest value is greater than 0 (`4294967295 0 0 1 4294967295`) | pass | E's comparison battery against C++ (lm1, 2026-11-06), reduced: char32_t(4294967295) > 0 is false in kpp on every target, true in clang, MSVC and g++ |  |
| `language/char32_t_promotes_to_unsigned_int.kpp` | char32_t promotes to unsigned int, as int cannot hold its values, and the other character types to int (C++ [conv.prom] 2): given f(int) and f(unsigned), f(U'a') calls f(unsigned), a promotion beating a conversion, and f(u'a'), f(L'a') and f(u8'a') call f(int); an unscoped enumeration over char32_t promotes as char32_t does; `U'a' + 1` is an unsigned int and `-U'a'` 4294967199, at compile time too (clang gives the same) | pass |  |  |
| `language/char_array_from_string_literal.kpp` | a char array initialised by a string literal: the literal's characters and its terminating zero, the rest of the array zero; without a length the array takes the literal's (`char s[] = "abc"` has four elements) | pass | Crypto.cpp's `static const char alphanum[] = "0123456789ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz";` |  |
| `language/char_array_from_string_literal_in_braces.kpp` | a string literal in braces initialises an array of characters as one without them (C++ [dcl.init.string]): `uint8_t text[] = {"abc"}` has four elements, the literal's characters and its terminating zero; a brace list of one character is still one element, an array of pointers one pointer | pass |  |  |
| `language/char_array_literal_forms.kpp` | a char array from a string literal in braces, a wchar_t array from L"..", a member's default char text[8] = "abc", beside an array of pointers from a list of literals (each element a pointer, untouched by the char rule) | pass |  |  |
| `language/closure_copies_and_destroys_captures.kpp` | a closure that captures objects by value copies them through their copy constructors when it is copied, moves them when moved, and destroys them when it dies: as a variable, a temporary, a parameter by value, and inside a Function | pass | EntityWithOwner's loading constructor pushes `SimpleLoadHelper::Setup([entity = Targeter<EntityWithOwner>(this)] { ... })`: the closure was copied bitwise into the helper and the one on the stack was never destroyed, so its targeter stayed in the entity's list after the constructor returned, and loading the menu maps crashed in Targeters::clearTargetersWithType walking into the dead stack |  |
| `language/condition_declaration_of_reference.kpp` | a condition that declares a reference, `if (const Connector& c = p.spoil)`, tests the object the reference names (its explicit operator bool), not the reference's address | pass | RecipePrototype::addTrashRecursive's `if (const IDConnector<ItemID>& spoilResultID = itemID->spoilResult)`: always true, so it followed every item's empty spoil result and `--dump-data` crashed reading the prototype of an empty ID |  |
| `language/condition_declaration_or_expression.kpp` | an if/while condition that reads like a declaration but is an expression (`p && f(p)`, `a & mask(a)`), next to real condition declarations (`T x = e`, `T x{e}`) and init-statements with a parenthesized initializer | pass | `if (entity && entity->isValid())`-style conditions, everywhere |  |
| `language/conditional_in_generic_lambda_takes_both_branch_types.kpp` | a conditional one of whose branches the checker cannot type in a generic body (a recursive generic lambda's `self(self, n - 1)`, a long long or a double) has the type C++'s usual arithmetic conversions give both branches: `n <= 1 ? 1 : n * self(self, n - 1)` is a long long (20! whole), so is `n <= 1 ? 1 : self(self, n - 1)` stored in a local, and `n == 0 ? 1 : self(self, n - 1) / 2` a double (0.125, not 0); clang prints the same | pass |  |  |
| `language/conditional_mixed_class.kpp` | `cond ? "text" : someString` has the class type when one branch converts to the other's class | pass |  |  |
| `language/conditional_of_class_and_number.kpp` | `c ? object : number` where the object's class converts to a number (`operator double()`, an inherited `operator const T&()`) and the number converts to no object of the class: the conditional has the number's type (C++ tries each way and takes the one that works), the object's value converted, then the usual arithmetic conversions between the two numbers | pass | FlyingRobotPrototype::addAdjustedEnergyConsumptionToDescription's `forceData ? forceData->workerRobotsBatteryModifier : 0.0` (SimpleModifierTarget's operator double), ClearCursorLogic::execute's `cursorID ? UtilityConstants::instance().clearCursorVolumeModifier : 1.0f` (Const<float>'s inherited operator const float&), MapPreviewGenerator's `threadCount == 0 ? global->otherSettings->maxMapPreviewThreads : threadCount` (SimpleConfigItem<uint8_t>'s operator const uint8_t& beside a uint32) |  |
| `language/conditional_of_lvalues.kpp` | `c ? a : b` of two lvalues of one type is an lvalue (assignable, forwarded as `const T&` when they are const members), and a const scalar prvalue binds an `Args&&` (a prvalue of a scalar type is never const) | pass | DrawQueue's `create<SpriteDrawOrder>(..., shadow ? shift.second : shift.first, ...)` and Animation's `stripes.emplace_back(fileName, parameters.lineLength != 0 ? parameters.lineLength : parameters.frameCount, ...)` with const parameters |  |
| `language/conditional_of_pointers.kpp` | `c ? a : b` of arrays or pointers: the arrays decay to pointers (also against nullptr), and two pointers meet at their composite pointer type (to the more qualified pointee, to void, to the base class), whichever branch is first; two arrays of one type stay an lvalue of that array | pass | ZipPackage's `unzGetCurrentFileInfo64(..., fileNameInfo ? fileNameInfo->buffer : nullptr, ...)` (a char array member against nullptr) |  |
| `language/conditional_of_two_arrays.kpp` | a conditional whose both arms are arrays of one type, `y != 0 ? mapping : firstmap`, is that array (an lvalue, as in C++), and it decays to a pointer to its first element where one is wanted: a pointer initialized from it points into the array chosen; also arrays of one element type and different lengths, which decay in the arms (a pointer) | pass | stb_image_write (converted as C): stbiw__encode_png_line's `int *mymap = (y != 0) ? mapping : firstmap;` of two `static int[5]`, the PNG filter of each row |  |
| `language/conditional_of_two_classes.kpp` | `c ? a : b` of two different classes has the type the other branch converts to (C++ tries each way): a value and an Optional of it is the Optional, a derived and a base object is the base, whichever branch is first | pass | UndoItem::mergeRailPlannerUndos's `return first && second ? | `language/conditional_prvalue_initializes_directly.kpp` | a conditional whose branches are prvalues of the variable's class initializes the variable directly ([expr.cond]/7, [dcl.init]/17.6.1: guaranteed copy elision), no temporary moved from; clang 19 prints `T1`, `~T1`, `0` | pass | found by the templates thread comparing object lifetimes against clang 19 (an extra move constructor call and destructor, observable where they have effects) |  |
| `language/conditional_with_throw_branch.kpp` | a conditional with a throw-expression as one branch (C++'s [expr.cond]/2): `x > 0 ? x : throw x` is the other branch, of its type, in a return and an initializer, the throw in either branch, of a class type too; a void one stays void; until 2026-11-06 a conversion from void was an error | pass |  |  |
| `language/const_before_specifier_qualifies_the_base_type.kpp` | a `const` written before a storage class or an elaborated type specifier qualifies the base type, as one written before the type does: `const static char* name` is a `const char*`, `const struct S* p` a `const S*`, not a const pointer ([dcl.type]/[dcl.spec]: the decl-specifiers are in any order) | pass | TestLuaEventFilters's `const static char* controlLua = R"(...)"` and SocketAddress's `const struct sockaddr_in* addr4 = (const struct sockaddr_in*)(...)` were typed `char* const` and `sockaddr_in* const` (silent: the pointee was writable, the pointer not), found by the templates thread checking pointer conversions |  |
| `language/const_lambda_variable.kpp` | a lambda held by a const variable (`const auto f = [this](int) {...}`), called and passed on | pass | 52 uses in 31 files |  |
| `language/const_member_function_members_are_const.kpp` | in a const member function the object is const ([class.this] 1: `this` is a `const T*`), and so is every data member named through it: written alone (`v = 1;`, `++count;`), with its class (`Base::b = 2;`), an array member's element (`this->cells[0] = 5;`, `cells[1] = 6;`), in a lambda that captures this; writing one or calling a non-const member function on it (`items.push_back(1)`) is a compile error, as in C++; a mutable member may change, and what a pointer member points at is no part of the object | pass |  |  |
| `language/const_pointer_to_function.kpp` | a const pointer to a function, `int (*const fixed)(int) = twice;`, a local, a global and a member, and a const pointer to an array, `int (*const row)[3] = grid;`, declare (a local was read as an expression until 2026-11-06: "unexpected keyword in expression"); the pointer is const: assigning it is an error (const_pointer_to_function_assigned_is_error) | pass |  |  |
| `language/const_pointer_to_function_assigned_is_error.kpp` | a const pointer to a function cannot be assigned, as C++ has it | pass |  |  |
| `language/const_value_assigned.kpp` | a const value is never assigned: `r = 5` through a `const int&`, a const local's `c += 1`, a `const volatile` local's `cv = 4` and `cb.get() = 7` through a const getter are errors, as in C++ | pass | found by the templates thread with language/non_const_member_overload_for_non_const_object: `history[key] = 1` through the const operator[]'s `const int&` was accepted and stored (silent); the check flagged 101 valid game lines until `volatile T` stopped being typed `const volatile T` (language/volatile_is_not_const) |  |
| `language/const_value_incremented.kpp` | a const value is never incremented, decremented or assigned as a whole: `++c`, `c--` on a const int, `++*p` through a `const int*` and the implicit copy assignment of a const object are errors, as in C++ ([expr.pre.incr], [class.copy.assign]) | pass | found by the templates thread with language/const_value_assigned: each was accepted and stored (silent) |  |
| `language/constant_calls_plain_function_defined_later.kpp` | a constant may call a plain function defined after it (kpp's symbols are order-independent): the evaluator runs the function as a walk of it types it, made for the evaluation when its file's walk has not reached it yet (or is in it on another thread), so `pick(v)` is pick(int), never the first pick of the name | pass | found by the templates thread answering thread A (are plain functions safe to evaluate while their file is walked?): before, the evaluator read the function's own nodes, unresolved above their walk, and ran the overload set's first pick, so `int sized[twice(3)]` had 200 elements while twice(3) is 6 |  |
| `language/constant_data_globals.kpp` | a global whose initializer the compiler evaluates is constant data, built by no startup code: a class with a constexpr constructor, bit fields, an aggregate holding an array of classes, a string and a double, a pointer to another global and to a function, an array, a pointer into it; each reads back its value | pass |  |  |
| `language/constant_reads_initializer_defined_later.kpp` | a constant read before its definition, or from another file (kpp's symbols are order-independent), is the value of its initializer as a walk of it types it: `pick(3)` in k's initializer is pick(int), in a lambda called there, in a data member's default and in an enumerator's value too, whatever order the files are walked in | pass | found by the templates thread checking compile-time lambdas for thread A: with k = pick(3) in another file, `int sized[k]` had 100 or 4 elements by the order the files were walked in and the thread count; the evaluator read the initializer's own nodes, unresolved above their file's walk |  |
| `language/constant_template_calls.kpp` | a constant expression calling a function template without written template arguments (`max(a, b)`: deduced from the arguments' types), and a type's name called with one value (`size_t(5)` through an alias, `uint8_t(300)`), evaluate as template arguments and in static_asserts | pass | PropertyTree's `using KeyType = ImmutableStringTemplate<::max(sizeof(SharedPtr<const String>) + 1u, 2 * sizeof(const char *) - 1)>` |  |
| `language/consteval_address_of_element.kpp` | at compile time `&v[i]` of a container's element (Vector's, Array's operator[]) is the element's address where the run holds it: read and written through, indexed, compared, as at run time (`5 9 5 1` both) | pass |  |  |
| `language/consteval_addresses.kpp` | addresses at compile time: of a global, of an element of one, of a function (written with & or decaying), and a pointer to a data member; read through, compared, indexed, called through | pass | function pointer tables (zlib_filefunc64_def_s's fopen64_file_func), RailPrototype's constant accessor arrays of member pointers |  |
| `language/consteval_aggregate_from_derived_slices.kpp` | at compile time an aggregate B made from an object of a class derived from it copies that object's B part (slicing, B's implicit copy constructor), as at run time: `B b2 = makeD();`, `B(makeD())`, a member of an aggregate from a D prvalue or a D constant (`Out{makeD(), 5}`, `Out{d, 7}`) (clang gives the same) | pass |  |  |

| `language/consteval_assignment_operator_runs_on_both_objects.kpp` | a class's assignment operator at compile time runs on the assigned object where it is, with the right operand where it is (an lvalue's object, a prvalue as a temporary): a move assignment that changes its source changes it, and `this == &other` compares the two objects; the constant equals what the program computes (`1 1 2`) | pass | found by the templates thread measuring build.kpp (stage 7): String's `operator=(String&&)` compares `this == &other`, and its move must empty the source (else two Strings own one block) |  |

| `language/inlined_trivial_functions.kpp` | a call of a trivial function (one statement `return E;` or `E;` over its parameters, `this` and fields, built-in operators only) behaves exactly as the call: the arguments are evaluated once and in order, a reference parameter and a reference result stay references, `this` reached through a second base is that base's subobject, a setter and an increment write through, and a call that does not inline (an overloaded operator inside) sits beside them unchanged (docs/compile-speed.md, "Design: inlining trivial functions in kppc"; `--no-inline` keeps every call) | pass |  |  |

| `language/nodiscard_and_deprecated_warn.kpp` | a call whose result an expression statement discards is a warning where the function is [[nodiscard]], or the class or enumeration it returns is, and a call of a [[deprecated]] function is a warning with the reason it was given, as C++ compilers warn; a cast to void says the discard is meant, and a result kept or a function without the attribute is no warning; a warning is no error (the program builds and runs); in a template instance's body it is a warning where the instance is made, Box<Probe>::refresh, called, but not in Box<Probe>::touch, which nothing calls, nor in the primary template's body for the argument an explicit specialization takes, instances C++ never makes (clang warns alike) | pass |  |  |
| `language/noexcept_false_destructor_throws_to_its_caller.kpp` | a destructor declared `noexcept(false)` may throw, and its exception leaves the scope whose end ran it as any other: a try around the scope catches it, and the code after the try runs on ([except.spec]/9, [except.ctor]); clang 19 prints `5 then 6` | pass | eleven `noexcept(false)` destructors that throw by design (ChartedChunksEventHelper's "because sendToListeners can throw", RollingStock::CarriageGuard, ForceManager, MapSerialiser, ...) |  |
| `language/noexcept_false_destructor_unwinds_what_was_built_before.kpp` | a destructor that throws leaves the objects built before its object to be destroyed, and nothing after it twice: the locals of the scope, a class's members and bases, a temporary, `delete`, a destructor whose own body throws (its members are destroyed), a loop's iteration, and a handler's local (the exception leaves the handler to the try around it) ([except.ctor]/2, [class.dtor]/13); clang 19 prints the same | pass | the eleven `noexcept(false)` destructors (ChartedChunksEventHelper, RollingStock::CarriageGuard, ForceManager, MapSerialiser, ...); a throw from one during another exception's unwinding ends the program (std::terminate), as in C++ |  |
| `language/noexcept_function_with_locals.kpp` | a noexcept function with locals to destroy: when nothing throws, its locals are destroyed as usual; when an exception reaches it, the program ends through the terminate handler from the search, its locals and the thrower's not destroyed (the cleanups' unwinding and the noexcept function's stop, on every target) | pass |  |  |
| `language/noexcept_specifier_takes_its_value.kpp` | a noexcept specifier with an expression (`noexcept(false)`, `noexcept(isNothrowDestructible<T>)`, `noexcept(sizeof(int) == 4)`) is noexcept only when its constant is true, as in C++ ([except.spec]/2): the nothrow traits read that, and a `noexcept(false)` function is potentially-throwing; clang 19 prints `0 1 1 0 1` | pass | FlatMap's move constructor `noexcept(std::is_nothrow_move_constructible_v<value_type>)`, UniquePointer's `noexcept(is_nothrow_destructible)`, and eleven `noexcept(false)` destructors (ChartedChunksEventHelper's "because sendToListeners can throw") |  |
| `language/noexcept_violation_terminates_at_the_throw.kpp` | an exception leaving a noexcept function ends the program through the terminate handler (C++'s std::terminate, [except.terminate]), found in the search before anything unwinds: the throw site is still on the stack, so no destructor between the throw and the noexcept function runs and no handler outside it is reached (kovarex, 2026-11-03: "we need to be able to report crashes like that") | pass |  |  |
| `language/non_const_member_overload_for_non_const_object.kpp` | for a non-const object the non-const member overload wins when the arguments rank the same (`history[key]` with `const T& operator[](StringView) const` and `T& operator[](const String&)`, the key a `const char*`: both arguments are user-defined conversions, and the implicit object parameter decides) | pass | ModManager::addHistoryForFont's `this->prototypeHistory[FontBank::type][name].add(...)` called PrototypesHistoryTemplate's const lookup instead of the inserting operator[], which aborted with "No history for the given key: font" in FontBank::setupDefaultFonts (the first crash of `factorio --dump-data`) |  |
| `language/nonconst_reference_binds_lvalue_of_its_type.kpp` | a non-const lvalue reference parameter binds an lvalue of its own type, or of a class derived from it, only ([dcl.init.ref] 5): given an int lvalue for a `double&`, a Box for a `Holder&`, a string literal for a `String&`, a brace list for a `Point&`, or a class whose conversion operator gives a value (`operator T*()`) for a `T*&`, the call is a compile error, as in C++, never a temporary the function changes in the argument's stead; a `const T&` takes all of them, `T&` an lvalue of T or of a derived class, and a class whose conversion operator gives an lvalue (`operator T*&()`, Agui's ValueComparePointer) binds it | pass |  |  |
| `language/nonconst_reference_binds_no_const_object.kpp` | a non-const lvalue reference binds no const object, whatever binds it: a variable (`int& r = c;` with `const int c`, `S& t = s;` with `const S s`, `int& e{c};`), a function's result (`int& get() const { return this->v; }`, a const member function's member being const) and a range-for variable over const elements (`for (int& x : numbers)` with `const Vector<int> numbers`) are compile errors, as in C++ ([dcl.init.ref] 5; clang: "binding reference of type 'int' to value of type 'const int' drops 'const' qualifier"); a `const T&` binds each, and a `T&` an object that is not const | pass |  |  |
| `language/nonconst_reference_binds_no_value.kpp` | a non-const lvalue reference binds no value, a prvalue or an xvalue, as in C++ ([dcl.init.ref] 5; clang: "non-const lvalue reference to type 'int' cannot bind to a temporary of type 'int'"): `int& r = value();` with value() returning int, `int& m = move(x);` and `return 5;` from a function returning int& are compile errors; a `const int&` binds the value (its temporary lives as long as the reference), an `int&` binds the lvalue a call returns (`int& g = global();`), and a `Gen&` the conditional of two lvalues, one of them a call returning `Gen&` (`c ? w.a : w.getB()`) | pass |  |  |
| `language/nonconst_reference_to_other_type_is_error.kpp` | a non-const lvalue reference parameter binds only an object of its own type (or of a class derived from it), as in C++ ([dcl.init.ref] 5; clang: "non-const lvalue reference to type 'double' cannot bind to a value of unrelated type 'int'"): an int for a `double&`, a Box for a `Holder&` that Holder's constructor makes one of, a literal for a `String&` and a braced list for a `Point&` are errors each, where kpp passed the int's address as a double's (8 bytes written into 4: the program crashed) and made the others temporaries that the function changed instead of the argument | pass |  |  |

| `kpp/when_member_used_in_assert.kpp` | an assert's expression is the debug configuration's (kpp's assert is nothing, its expression unchecked, where config::debug is false: design.md 12), so it may name a `when (config::debug)` member or function (design.md 19): the release build compiles, and the debug one checks | pass | 38 of plan B's 46 release errors: `assert(!this->childrenBeingIterated)` (agui::Widget), `assert(this->getDirectInputLine() == this->directInput)` (TransportLine), `assert(!this->isBeingLoaded())` (Targeter), `assert(this->postLoadSetupRan)` (Trigger, ProductPrototype), `assert(this->isSetup)` (RequesterCounts), `assert(!this->mapped)` (VideoBufferOpenGL), each a member under #ifdef DEBUG in the C++ |  |

## C interop

| Snippet | Construct | Status | Factorio | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `interop/c_alloca_through_another_header.kpp` | _alloca is a function of the import wherever an imported header's malloc.h has it, also where malloc.h is not imported itself (another header includes it) and its _alloca is MinGW-w64's macro of __builtin_alloca (windows-gnu): a block on the calling function's stack, aligned to 16 bytes (`703 0`) | pass | MiningDrill, DrawQueue, Framebuffer and Noise call _alloca, which the game's import has through SDL_stdinc.h's malloc.h (MSVC) and MinGW-w64's stdlib.h (windows-gnu), never an import of malloc.h: the windows-gnu build had "'_alloca' is not declared" |  |
| `interop/c_anonymous_member_of_named_type.kpp` | MS C's anonymous member of a named struct type (`MONITORINFO;` inside MONITORINFOEXW, `SpecPoint2;`): as the first member, its members are the struct's and the layout is C's; anywhere else it is laid out, but its members are not reachable by their names | pass | GraphicsInterfaceDX11 and SDLWindow fill `MONITORINFOEX info; info.cbSize = sizeof(MONITORINFOEX);` for GetMonitorInfo: the import dropped the anonymous MONITORINFO (`MONITORINFO ;` read as a macro statement), so the struct was 64 bytes instead of 104 and cbSize no member (silent: GetMonitorInfo would have written past it) |  |
| `interop/c_callable_struct_by_value_through_pointer.kpp` | a C-callable function defined in kpp that takes a struct by value, called through a function pointer in kpp: the call passes the struct as the function takes it (as its target's C does), the pointer's type being the function's own, of C's calling convention (`auto`, decltype), as a call through a pointer to a C library's function does (interop/c_function_pointer_struct_by_value) | pass | none; until 2026-11-05 a call through a function pointer passed a struct as kpp does, and a function pointer's type did not say whose calling convention its function has: it read the registers of whatever came before (45 -1336165242 on Windows), and taking the address of such a function was refused (thread D's 088b6d2e) until function types of C's convention (thread B) |  |
| `interop/c_callable_structs_by_value.kpp` | a C-callable function defined in kpp (extern "C") takes and returns structs by value as its target's C passes them, as C calls it (c_callable_structs_by_value.c): MS x64 a struct of 1, 2, 4 or 8 bytes in a register and any other through memory; SysV x86-64 up to 16 bytes in INTEGER or SSE registers by its eightbytes, others on the stack (`byval`), on the stack whole when its registers ran out, a result in registers or through the caller's slot (`sret`); kpp's own calls of them alike (the C side's checks are numbered: 0 when all hold) | pass | on Linux, fontstash.kpp's renderer callbacks that take a struct by value (2 functions): "a C-callable function (extern "C") defined in kpp that takes or returns a struct by value" |  |
| `interop/c_callbacks.kpp` | kpp functions as C callbacks (function pointers with C signatures) and C function pointers stored in kpp | pass |  |  |
| `interop/c_class_through_varargs.kpp` | an object of a trivially copyable class passed through a C function's `...`, as MSVC passes one (C++ allows it): a class of 1, 2, 4 or 8 bytes by value, any other by the address of a copy; the C function's va_arg of the same struct reads it | pass | InputActionTestMaker's VariableToCode<QuickBarSetSlotParameters> and <QuickBarPickSlotParameters>, `ssprintf("...ItemStackIndex(%u))", param.targetQuickBarSlot)` with a 2-byte QuickBarSlotLocation, and PlatformBackdropCloud::parseCloudProjectionStyle's `ssprintf("\"%s\" is unknown ...", input)` with a std::string_view: wrong format arguments in the game (debug and error paths), which MSVC compiles |  |
| `interop/c_constant_through_function_macro.kpp` | an object-like macro whose body is a function-like macro's call on a constant is a constant too: SDL3's `#define SDL_WINDOW_FULLSCREEN SDL_UINT64_C(0x0000000000000001)` | pass | SDL's window flags at 6 uses (SDL_WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, SDL_WINDOW_MAXIMIZED, ImguiSDL's SDL_WINDOW_MOUSE_FOCUS) |  |
| `interop/c_cpuid_header.kpp` | cpuid.h's __get_cpuid_max, __get_cpuid and __get_cpuid_count, inline functions whose bodies are inline asm (`__asm("cpuid" : "=a"(...) ...)`), are the code generator's cpuid instruction, as MSVC's __cpuid is: the highest basic and extended leaves, leaf 0 answered (its eax the highest leaf, with or without a subleaf), and a leaf above the highest refused with 0, the four values left as they were (`true true true true true true`) | pass | SystemUtil's cpuid and getCpuidExtendedMaxInputValue on Linux (`#include <cpuid.h>`, MSVC's __cpuid on Windows): "codegen does not support the statement kind AsmStmt yet (in __get_cpuid(...))" |  |
| `interop/c_declspec_align.kpp` | `__declspec(align(N))` on a C struct (winnt.h's DECLSPEC_ALIGN: CONTEXT, M128A) is its alignment: alignof is N, and a local of it lands on N bytes (RtlCaptureContext stores into a CONTEXT with aligned SSE moves) | pass | StackWalker::ShowCallstack's local `CONTEXT c; RtlCaptureContext(&c);` crashed with an access violation reading 0xffffffffffffffff when the game logged a stack: the import aligned CONTEXT to 8 |  |
| `interop/c_file_is_the_c_runtimes.kpp` | the standard library's FILE is the C runtime's own (stdlib/clib): on Windows the UCRT's `struct _iobuf`, one pointer, so sizeof(FILE) is C's 8, and a stream through FILE* writes and reads back | pass | the game's FILE* streams (fopen, fwrite, fprintf to stderr) and the converted C libraries' (zlib's gzip files, minizip's) |  |
| `interop/c_float_limits.kpp` | float.h's limits through the import: FLT_MAX, FLT_EPSILON, DBL_MAX, DBL_EPSILON | pass | 8 uses (ImguiSDL's `ImVec2(-FLT_MAX, -FLT_MAX)`, AsteroidCollectorNavmeshPathfinding's `float pathCost = FLT_MAX;`, RocketSilo's FLT_EPSILON) |  |
| `interop/c_function_address_in_constant_data.kpp` | a constant table holding the address of a C-callable function the program defines (`extern "C"`), beside a kpp function's, is constant data every module of the build can name: a module that has the table's global declares the C function where another module defines it, as it does a kpp function | pass | Lua's linit.c `loadedlibs` ({"_G", luaopen_base}, ..., {NULL, NULL}) once its null entries are constant data as well (thread D, 2026-11-05: `use of undefined value '@luaopen_base'`); a program with such a table failed to build with more than one module ("clang rejected module 120"), the C function's name only in the shared global's initializer |  |
| `interop/c_function_pointer_conventions_differ_is_error.kpp` | a pointer to a function of C's calling convention that takes or returns a struct by value (a C function's, an extern "C" one's, a C header's pointer type) is another type than a function pointer written in kpp with the same signature, which passes the struct as kpp does: no conversion between the two, either way, where a call through it would pass the struct as the function does not take it; the error names both types (a function type of C's convention prints with extern C). A signature that passes no struct by value is one type for both | pass | none; a call through such a pointer passed the struct as kpp does (a silent wrong value: interop/c_function_pointer_struct_by_value) |  |
| `interop/c_function_pointer_parameter.kpp` | a pointer to a function of C's calling convention (an extern "C" function taking a struct by value) is a parameter of a kpp function and a template's argument like any other type, and a call through it there passes the struct as C does: the function's name, which holds its parameters' types, is a valid LLVM name (`45 45 4`) | pass | none; the C convention's first spelling, `extern "C" int32(S8)`, put quotes inside codegen's quoted LLVM names (`@"callIt(extern "C" int32(S8)*, S8)"`), which clang rejected |  |
| `interop/c_function_pointer_struct_by_value.kpp` | a call through a pointer to a C function that takes or returns a struct by value passes and receives it as the target's C does, as a direct call does: the pointer's function type is C's (the function's own, a C header's pointer type, a struct field a C header declares, filled in by C), whatever the struct's size: 8 bytes, 16 of doubles, 24 (`45 45 45`, `6 7 6 7`, `3 5 7.5`, `24 48`) | pass | none yet (the game calls its C functions directly); a call through such a pointer passed the struct as kpp passes one, a pointer to a copy, which the function read as its registers: a silent wrong value (thread D's 088b6d2e, the callee side, left this one) |  |
| `interop/c_gcc_builtins.kpp` | GCC's builtins that C code calls are the code generator's own, as MSVC's intrinsics are (msvcBuiltin), wherever C headers are imported: __builtin_clz and __builtin_ctz (unsigned int), the l forms (unsigned long, the target's C long), the ll forms (64 bits), __builtin_popcount (l, ll), __builtin_bswap16, 32 and 64, __builtin_expect (its first argument), __builtin_prefetch (one to three arguments) and __builtin_alloca (stack memory for the rest of the function) (`8 20 23 40 4 1 3 0 8`, `13330 2018915346 578437695752307201 7 42`) | pass | the game's Linux branches (`#ifdef __GNUC__`): __builtin_clz/ctz/clzll/ctzll in the bit utilities, __builtin_bswap32/64 in the serialiser, __builtin_prefetch and __builtin_alloca, which MSVC's _BitScanReverse, _byteswap_*, _mm_prefetch and _alloca are on Windows ("'__builtin_clz' is not declared") |  |
| `interop/c_header_inline_functions.kpp` | a function an imported C header defines inline (the UCRT's corecrt_math.h `__inline long double floorl(long double)`, `__inline float fabsf(float)`), which no library exports, is callable: the import compiles its body | pass | trio (converted as C): trio.c's trio_floor and trio_ceil are floorl and ceill (TRIO_FEATURE_FLOAT with long double), in TrioWriteDouble |  |
| `interop/c_header_null_pointer_spellings.kpp` | an integer literal of value zero is a null pointer constant wherever C code gives one to a pointer, in every spelling C has, its suffix u and l or ll in either order and any case (`0UL`, `0lu`, `0ull`), hex's `0x0` and octal's `00`: one rule (lexer.hpp's isZeroLiteral) for codegen, overload resolution's null arguments and the check that refuses the literal 0 for a pointer in kpp code (`0 0 0 0 0 1`) | pass | none; codegen's list and overload resolution's lacked `0ul` and `0UL`, which the zero-null check had ("codegen does not support passing unsigned long where a pointer is expected yet" for a C header's `f(0UL)`) |  |
| `interop/c_hresult_from_win32_is_a_function.kpp` | windows.h's HRESULT_FROM_WIN32 is a function of the import, as the Windows SDK has it (an inline function), also with MinGW-w64's headers, whose winerror.h makes it a function-like macro, which the import does not carry (windows-gnu) (`-2147024891 0 -5`) | pass | WICLoaderData (SpriteLoaders' WIC errors) calls HRESULT_FROM_WIN32, which kpp-native's inline_c.c makes clang emit for the link; the windows-gnu build had "'HRESULT_FROM_WIN32' is not declared" |  |
| `interop/c_import_anonymous_enum.kpp` | a C header's anonymous enum behind a typedef, `typedef enum { ... } CURLcode;`, as a type in kpp, also inside a struct's anonymous union (curl's CURLMsg.data.result) | pass |  |  |
| `interop/c_import_fixed_width_typedefs_are_kpps.kpp` | an imported header's typedef of a name kpp builds in (int64_t, uint64_t, size_t, ptrdiff_t, intptr_t, uintptr_t) is kpp's own type where the two agree in width and signedness, on every target: glibc's `typedef long int64_t;` and `typedef unsigned long size_t;` on LP64 targets made them C's long, a type of its own in kpp, so a C function taking int64_t* or size_t* refused kpp's i64* and usize* ("a pointer (int64*) does not convert to long*"), and kpp's own int64_t meant long wherever stdint.h was imported. One that differs in width stays the header's (glibc's 64-bit int_fast32_t), and C's long stays long (`3 5 7 9 11 13 15 17`, `3 7 8 8 true`) | pass | on Linux, SDL_StepUTF8(&text, &len) with `usize len` (size_t*), and glGetQueryObjecti64v(..., &value) with `i64 value` (GLint64*, khrplatform's int64_t) |  |
| `interop/c_import_leaves_out_library_functions.kpp` | an imported C function that the standard library defines at global scope as a function of its own, with the same name and parameter types, is left out of the import, so that a call finds the library's and is never ambiguous between the two (glibc's math.h declares the BSD functions `int isinf(double)` and `int isnan(double)` beside the library's `bool isinf(double)`; the UCRT's stdlib.h `int abs(int)` beside the library's); a C function the library only redeclares (`extern "C" double sqrt(double)`) is the same function and stays | pass | the game's math.h and stdlib.h imports: isinf and isnan on Linux, abs everywhere |  |
| `interop/c_import_member_alignment_attribute.kpp` | `__attribute__((aligned(N)))` written after an imported C struct member's declarator aligns that member: the struct's size and alignment are C's, and so are its members' places (the C side reads what kpp wrote) (`96 16 96 16 16 80 4321`) | pass | none; kpp.net's FreeBSD 14.3 headers: machine/ucontext.h's `long mc_fpstate[64] __attribute__((__aligned__(16)))` and struct sigcontext's sc_fpstate were dropped, so ucontext_t was 360 bytes, not 880 (B's review of I's FreeBSD target) |  |
| `interop/c_import_missing_header_is_error.kpp` | importing a C header the target does not have is an error named where the program imports it (`file:line`), each such header (clang's own message names only the import's wrapper file in the cache) | pass | the merged tree built for Linux (thread B, 2026-11-05): the common import files still named Windows' io.h, conio.h and direct.h, and the build said only "fatal error: 'io.h' file not found" at a line of kpp-imports\<hash>.c |  |
| `interop/c_import_nullability_restrict_noreturn.kpp` | clang's nullability qualifiers (`_Nonnull`, `_Nullable`, `_Null_unspecified`), C99's `restrict` before a parameter's name and C11's `_Noreturn` in an imported C header change nothing in a declaration: a struct keeps every member (its layout is C's) and the functions are declared (`32 10 12`) | pass | none; kpp.net's FreeBSD 14.3 headers have them: stdio.h's FILE (`int (* _Nullable _close)(void *)`), pthread.h's `pthread_mutex_t * restrict __mutex`, stdlib.h's `_Noreturn void exit(int)` (B's review of I's FreeBSD target) |  |

| `interop/c_import_typedef_of_bool_keyword.kpp` | an imported header's typedef that names C's _Bool keyword (stdbool.h's `#define bool _Bool` before a `typedef enum {...} bool;`, which C does not allow) is not declared: _Bool stays C's bool in every declaration of the import, so a `const bool*` a C function returns is a kpp `const bool*` (`true false true false 1`) | pass | InputState's `const bool* keys = SDL_GetKeyboardState(...)`: in the windows-gnu import, curl's curl_setup.h (no HAVE_BOOL_T) made `typedef enum { bool_false, bool_true } _Bool;`, and SDL's `const _Bool*` became a pointer to that enumeration ("a pointer (const _Bool*) does not convert to const bool*") |  |

## expressions

| Snippet | Construct | Status | Factorio | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `expressions/address_of_reference_cast_to_second_base.kpp` | the address of `static_cast<B&>(d)` with B a base of D that is not at offset 0 (D : A, B) is the address of d's B part, as `B& b = static_cast<B&>(d)` binds it; casting back down with `static_cast<D&>(b)` gives d again | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/ambiguous_call_is_error.kpp` | a call that two overloads take equally well is an error, as in C++, never one of them taken silently: pick(int64, int32) and pick(int32, int64) given two int32s | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/assignment_evaluates_right_operand_first.kpp` | C++17's sequencing (design.md, "Evaluation order"): an assignment's right operand, = and every compound one, built-in or a class's operator, is evaluated before its left ([expr.ass] 1, [over.match.oper] 2); a subscript, a shift, .* and ->*, and a call (the callee, the object of a member call) evaluate their left operand first ([expr.sub], [expr.shift], [expr.mptr.oper], [expr.call] 8); the expected line is clang 19's | pass | CompiledMapGenSettings::createContext's `ctx.numericConstants[key] = *value;` with value a pointer into the same FlatMap: the left operand's insertion shifted the entries before *value was read, so "numeric-constant" got its neighbour's 1 instead of starting_area_radius's 150 (CompiledMapGenSettings/NoiseExpressionCompilationContext, the one failing unit test of 4c178a5b915) |  |
| `expressions/assignment_right_operand_first_closure.kpp` | an assignment whose right operand, evaluated first, is a lambda's closure holding what cannot be copied (`h->fn = [q = move(p)]() mutable {...}`, the left operand a call of SharedPtr's operator->) moves the closure into the Function, never copies it | pass | stdlib Future.kpp's async, `state->deferred = [raw, callable = move(callable)]() mutable {...}` (ForkTask, ThreadedChunkedZipPackageWriteStream): found making the right operand of an assignment evaluate first |  |
| `expressions/assignment_right_operand_first_keeps_value_category.kpp` | an assignment's right operand evaluated before its left keeps what it is: a temporary or an xvalue is moved from (operator=(T&&)), an lvalue, a call returning T& included, is copied; a free operator+= and an enum's operator|= take it as well; an assignment used as an lvalue (`int& r = (values[nextIndex()] = right());`) evaluates its left operand once; and the left operand's evaluation sees what the right one did (an insertion at the front that moves the element the right operand read); the expected line is clang 19's | pass | the pattern of CompiledMapGenSettings::createContext's `ctx.numericConstants[key] = *value;` (a FlatMap insertion moving the entry value points to) |  |
| `expressions/base_pointer_beats_void_pointer.kpp` | a pointer to a class converted to a pointer to its base is better than one converted to void* (C++ [over.ics.rank] 4.4.2): info(const Base*) over info(const void*) given a const Derived*, whichever is declared first, by value and by reference | pass | OpenableObjectWrapper's getPrototypeInfoStatic(const PrototypeBase*, ...) beside getPrototypeInfoStatic(const void*, ...) given each prototype type (29 calls), and makeExtra(const Sprite*) beside makeExtra(const void*) given a const LayeredSprite* |  |
| `expressions/c_cast_of_functional_cast.kpp` | a C-style cast to a type named by an alias or a class, `(S64)`, casts a functional cast or brace initialisation that follows it, whatever starts it: a builtin type `(S64)char(7)`, `(S64)unsigned(9)`, `decltype(x)(y)`, `typename T::value_type(v)`, a class `(S64)Meters{5}.value`; and an `alignof` expression; none of these can continue a parenthesised expression | pass | D's value corpus (generated expressions checked against clang): 42 cases of `(S64)char(7)` and its kin failed to parse ("expected ';' after declaration") |  |
| `expressions/c_ellipsis_is_the_worst_conversion.kpp` | matching a C ellipsis (`f(...)`, `f(int, ...)`) is worse than any standard or user-defined conversion ([over.ics.rank] 2): an int takes `f(FromInt)` (a converting constructor), `f(double)` and `f(const Base&)` for a Derived over `f(...)`; clang 19 prints `1 2 2 2` | pass | found by the templates thread comparing overload choices against clang 19 on generated calls: the ellipsis ranked as a standard conversion, so `f(...)` won against a user-defined one and tied with the others (silent and loud) |  |
| `expressions/class_to_scalar_without_conversion_is_error.kpp` | a class where a scalar is expected, an argument for a parameter that is no class or a copy-initialized variable's initializer, is the checker's error when the class has no implicit conversion operator to a scalar: an Item^ (a UniquePtr<Item>) for an int or an Item* parameter, a member function's included, or initializing an int or an Item* (C++ converts a class only through its conversion operators; the code generator stopped there as at a construct not supported yet) | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/conditional_converts_pointer_operand_to_class.kpp` | a conditional expression whose operands are a String and a `const char*` converts the pointer operand to String (the only direction an implicit conversion goes, C++ [expr.cond]): `none ? none->name : Mod::CORE_MOD` is the String "core" when none is null, passed to a `const String&` or used to initialize a String | pass | LuaGameScript::luaGetScriptInventories' `pushInventories(item.first ? item.first->id.name : Mod::CORE_MOD, &item.second)`: the core inventories went in under an empty name, so `game.get_script_inventories().core` was nil (LuaInventory LuaScriptInventoryOwnership) |  |
| `expressions/conditional_of_derived_and_base_is_the_base_part.kpp` | `c ? both : alone` with lvalues of a class Both and of its second base Second (Both : First, Second) is an lvalue of Second: in the first branch, both's Second part, read and assigned through a `Second&` bound to the conditional; and `c ? none : undo` with an Optional<Undo> and an Undo lvalue is a new Optional<Undo>, the Undo converted to it (C++ [expr.cond] 4); a base never converts to the derived class through the derived's converting constructor: `c ? negated : plain` with Negated deriving from Expression and `implicit Negated(const Expression&)` is plain itself when c is false (NoiseExpressions::Folding::subtract) | pass |  |  |

U200 : 0.1f` gave 0), and a const operand beside a plain one of its type made an int (TagManager's `player ? player->index : PlayerIndex(-1)`) |  |
| `expressions/enum_logical_operator_overload.kpp` | `a || b` and `a && b` of a scoped enumeration with its own operator|| and operator&& call those functions (both operands evaluated, the function's result), not the built-in short-circuit operators | pass | agui::Style's `PropertyStatus operator||(PropertyStatus, PropertyStatus)` (Style.kpp's addChangedValues), found by codegen's cross-check against the checker's recorded operator choices (design.md 24) |  |
| `expressions/enum_promotes_to_its_fixed_underlying_type.kpp` | an unscoped enumeration with a fixed underlying type promotes to that type (C++ [conv.prom] 4), which beats a conversion to another integer type: push(const int32&) beside push(const uint32&) given an `enum LuaEventType : uint32_t` takes the uint32 one; one without a fixed type promotes to int32 | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/enum_with_fixed_underlying_type_promotes_as_it.kpp` | an unscoped enumeration with a fixed underlying type promotes as that type does ([conv.prom]/4): an `enum : long long` stays 64 bits in arithmetic (`SM + 1` is a long long, `SM ^ 100u` a long long -97, not an unsigned int), an `enum : unsigned char` becomes an int (`U200 << 1` is 400, not 144); one without a fixed type is an int (MSVC's underlying type); clang 19 prints `8 -97 400 4 8` | pass | found by the templates thread comparing values against clang 19 on generated expressions: the resolver promoted every enumeration to int, codegen an `enum : uint8_t` to its uint8_t unpromoted (silent: other values) |  |
| `expressions/free_operator_against_builtin.kpp` | a free operator competes with the built-in operator on a class operand's conversion as C++ ranks them, operand by operand: `n + 1.0` with `operator+(const Other&, const Other&)` reachable through converting constructors is the built-in addition (its 1.0 needs no conversion), `-n` with `operator-(const Num&)` the free one (it binds n as it is) | pass | none; B's review of the free unary operators (778f2ad0) |  |
| `expressions/free_operator_beside_conversion_is_ambiguous.kpp` | a free operator taking a class operand only by a user-defined conversion (another class's converting constructor) beside the built-in operator on the class's own conversion operator (an explicit operator bool for `!`) is ambiguous, as C++ ranks the two: an error, neither one called | pass | none; B's review of the free unary operators (778f2ad0), which called the free one |  |
| `expressions/free_unary_operator.kpp` | a unary operator declared as a free function (`Coin operator-(const Coin&)`, `bool operator!(const Flag&)`) is called for a class operand without one of its own, its operand evaluated once | pass | none (the game's unary operators are members); found with hidden friends, which are free functions |  |
| `expressions/function_declared_twice_is_one_candidate.kpp` | a function declared more than once is one candidate, so a call of it is no ambiguity: a declaration and then its definition, a namespace's function declared in one block and defined in another, a member declared in its class and defined outside it, and a C function that a program declares again (the library declares strlen too) | pass |  |  |
| `expressions/function_pointer_of_other_type_is_error.kpp` | a pointer to a function initialises or assigns only a function pointer of its own type (no implicit conversion between function pointer types), as in C++ | pass | found checking the library's C declarations against windows.h: `void (*)(void**)` took the address of a `void (int*)` function |  |
| `expressions/integer_to_pointer_is_error.kpp` | an integer converts to a pointer without a cast only as the literal 0, the null pointer ([conv.ptr]/1): `int* p = 5;`, `int* q = n;`, `p = n;`, an int argument and an int returned for a pointer are errors; `int* z = 0;`, `z = 0;` and `(int*)n` are not | pass | found by the templates thread with expressions/null_pointer_argument_for_two_pointer_overloads_is_ambiguous: an initialisation and an assignment took any integer as an address (silent); an argument was codegen's unsupported construct |  |
| `expressions/literal_zero_through_pointer_constructor.kpp` | the literal 0 is a null pointer constant, so it reaches a class through a converting constructor taking a pointer, as nullptr does (a user-defined conversion): `f(FromText)` given 0 calls `FromText(const char*)` with a null pointer, and wins over a candidate 0 does not convert to; clang 19 prints `2 1` | pass | found by the templates thread comparing overload choices against clang 19 on generated calls (loud: no call) |  |
